## Chapter 11: Continuing with Frantic Concise

### Notes

#### Chapter Summary

The Siege of the North. Or as we like to call it, the Siege of Happiness.

#### Chapter Notes

I hate how this turned out ngl. been super stressed and insecure for the past couple of weeks, so I put this off (which is pretty stupid considering how much I LIKE writing this, but whatever brain)

anyway it's kinda long, so buckle up, grab a snack, a water, a blankie and enjoy. love ya

### Text

*Screeeeeeeeeech!*

Zuko gasped and shot up, immediately crying out in pain and hunching into himself. The first thing he noticed was the irritatingly blinding light that seemed to charge at him from every angle, but he quickly hid from it by shoving his head between his knees. Every twitch his fingers made felt restrictive, and he couldn’t feel most of his limbs. When he tried squeezing his eyes shut, discomfort laced up from his lip to his hairline. Instinctively, he shoved his hands underneath his armpits, concealing a gasp of pain at the movement by biting his lip.

Which also hurt.

Curled into a ball, on the borderline of hyperventilation, he tried willing a flame out of his breath. For the first few tries, nothing came out, causing his little amount of vision to shake and his brain to panic.

A timid flash of orange escaped his huffing, forcing him to fall into the habit of controlling his breath at the sight. He still felt trapped in his own body, every breath feeling like a large hand trying to find out how much pressure he could take before breaking. But as the flame grew bigger, the less it hurt to move his fingers, and the more his brain could comprehend.

A noise was ringing in Zuko’s ear, and it certainly wasn’t the screech that woke him up.

*Wait, screech?!*

Panting a bit from the pain that refused to recede, Zuko turned and tried to get his good eye to adjust to the light. Something was definitely messing with his usual pathetic excuse for eyesight, but he could see nonetheless.

“M… Momo?” Zuko exhaled, suddenly losing all energy he had, flopping back onto the snow and yelping at the shot of pain that struck his torso.

The lemur slowly hefted itself onto Zuko’s chest, crouching and leaning its head to the side. Zuko stared at it.

“What the *fuck…* just happened?” He groaned. He kept sucking in short breaths as if it would quell his urge to yell and hurt himself further.

*A ball of fire, shattering… cold.*

Zuko scrambled to stand, Momo flying off him with a frustrated squeak, and he told himself to ignore the pain. After the world stopped spinning, he glanced around.

Soggy paper floated in the water around the small patch of ice he floated on, which was covered in snow. Red canister-shaped items littered the area, next to little patches of black that must’ve been made from fire. With a grunt, Zuko bent to pick up a shell.

Explosives. Most of was dynamite.

The red stick had a soggy sheet of parchment clinging to it. Despite the warped ink, Zuko’s breath painfully caught at the familiar sight.

If the poster wasn’t torn and warped, it would’ve said ‘Wanted’ at the top. The spirit-like features, notwithstanding being split, were easily recognizable.

That ship in the ice, it wasn’t Fire Nation. Dynamite wasn’t used on Fire Nation issued ships, due to the hundreds of dangers of having it onboard.

Zuko had seen the ship before. And there was only one type of sailor that would be stupid enough to keep dynamite on a boat.

Pirates. Zuko crushed the paper in his fist, a scowl making whatever injuries he had on his face prickle.

*It all made sense!* Zuko thought, *Zhao said he’d sent a scouting ship… that isn’t standard protocol* *for an invasion or siege! Of course, he’d work with pirates, sending them to kill the Blue Spirit. He* *sent the posters out to bounty hunters. They must’ve seen my mask through the darkness… and this* *is the most sloppily-made projectile I’ve ever seen. Definitely pirates.*

A ball of explosives wrapped in his own wanted posters, set on fire. What a way to get blown up.

*That’s* why his ribs hurt so much. Zuko reached to his face, feeling a few cuts on his lip and above his eyebrow. Nothing hit his scar at least, but there was a bruise close to his good eye that was only off by a little bit. It could’ve easily become a black eye, but it wasn’t quite there. *The cuts were* *probably from ice shards* , Zuko noted.

Surprised, he finally noticed the weight of his bags on his back, *wow I’m great at tying those* *things,* but also bristled at the fact that his mask was missing. It took a few minutes of looking, but eventually, he found the mask stuck in the snow, only one of its eyes being slightly stained with soot. He sighed and shoved it into his bag.

Momo, who had been following him at the heel, climbed up his shoulder. Zuko blinked, just now seeing the paper attached to the blue ribbon that resided around the lemur’s neck.

Taking and unfurling it, he read;

*Dude, are you okay? It’s been a few days.*

*-Sokka*

Straight to the point. For once.

Alarms suddenly blared in Zuko’s head.

*I have to warn them.*

He turned to the horizon, the sun just hanging above the sea. It was morning.

In the east, a brewing cloud of black and gray festered. Glancing back at the mess surrounding him, he determinedly threw down the note with the rest of the scattered, gross paper. Momo squawked, and Zuko’s mouth set into a hard line.

The whole fleet of Zhao’s ships would take a few days to get through all the ice. Zuko could go faster.

After all, a few fractured ribs were hardly the worst obstacle he’d ever had to face.

He had to keep moving.

---

It was the dead of night. His injuries didn’t bother him much anymore, except for his ribs, which might’ve hurt more than they originally did. That’s what traveling all day without stopping will do.

Zuko stared up at the wall as if it had personally offended him, which it might as well have.

He hid behind a jagged structure of ice as a guard looked over the wall. Zuko knew he had to sneak in. They wouldn’t believe him if he just yelled like a madman, he needed to go to Aang directly. Zuko was *not* about to just walk in with the Fire Nation a mile behind him. That would look… pretty bad.

He also didn’t have any lychee nuts, so Momo barely listened to him. So, letters were a no.

Zuko traveled deeper into a crevice where the wall met an iceberg. It smelled like old fish, probably because of the turtle-seals that hung out there.

“Momo,” He whispered, “Momo!”

The lemur, somehow managing to have an attitude, sauntered over to Zuko. The firebender plopped his bags onto the animal, to which it screeched indignantly and struggled to wiggle out from underneath the weight.

“What, you can’t carry some bags over a wall but you can fly a few miles to wake me up?”

Momo crossed his arms, which really shouldn’t have been possible, but Zuko knew better than to question it.

“I don’t have any more lychee nuts! I have some toran, do you like that?”

The lemur seemed to consider it.

“Okay…” Zuko sighed, “I’ll give you all my toran and dried squid if you take my bags over the ice. Then you can go to Sokka, who will give you more food! And they’ll know that I’m on my way...”

Momo jumped around and chittered around, and Zuko took that as an affirmation. He emptied out his small bags of food, and while Momo ate he fastened his bags onto the small creature. Hopefully, Aang wouldn’t get too mad he was using his lemur as a transportation cart.

Momo finished his food and started to fly off. Zuko winced at how he wobbled at the weight, but once the lemur was over the wall, his mind went back into focus.

He pressed his palm to the wall and pushed heat forward. Not even a dent was made, the ice was way too thick. The turtle-seals barking annoyed him more than his lack of ideas.

“Would you just shut up?!” Zuko yelled, whipping around to face some blank-faced turtle-seals.

A few just went back to what they were doing after a few seconds of silence. However, some headed further in their crevice. Some desperate part of Zuko’s brain told him to follow them.

They led him to a cave, which he ducked to enter. In the cave, the gross fish smell intensified. Zuko concluded this is where the turtle-seals rested, but a splash caught his attention.

Tunnels. These turtle-seals came in and out of tunnels, but where did they end up? A tunnel always has two ends… and turtle-seals needed to come up for air eventually.

Zuko’s face tightened, dipping his legs into the unbearably freezing water.

Before submerging himself, he thought, *I never want to feel cold again after this.*

---

Sokka watched as the steam from the cold night air fizzled out from his mouth to nothingness. He wouldn’t admit it, but he liked these kinds of nights, where he could walk around with friends because they all couldn’t sleep. There was something oddly peaceful about it.

“... And then I actually landed the move! The shock on his face was hilarious!” Katara squealed. Yue giggled and Sokka sent her what his sister would describe as a ‘lovestruck’ look. He wasn’t lovestruck! It’s just that Yue was really pretty… no, beautiful… no, gorgeous-! But she and Sokka were just friends! He needed to stop thinking like that…

“Sokka!” Aang shouted, waving his hands in front of Sokka’s face, “I asked if Lee responded yet?”

“Oh, um,” Sokka sighed with defeat, “He hasn’t. I… only sent him a short note. I can’t believe he just ran into the freezing… middle of nowhere without any thought! He’s so…”

“Impulsive?” Offered Katara.

“Spontaneous?” Aang said.

“Bold?” Yue inquired.

“Insane,” Sokka groaned.

Yue raised her eyebrows. “Everything you told me about this boy makes me think he has no regard for his… personal safety.”

“That’s our Lee…” Aang grinned, “Did we tell you about the time he beat seven pirates and their captain at once?”

“Pirates?” Yue exclaimed, surprised.

“Ah yeah, he pretty much broke the captain’s wrist,” Sokka recalled.

Katara crossed her arms, “He saved our lives... more than once, actually. Don’t know how he still manages to act like an idiot though.”

“That’s one thing Lee and I have in common,” Sokka pointed out. Yue giggled.

What? He was willing to make himself seem like a fool if it meant she laughed.

“Well, Momo’s not back, so maybe he’s still writing back!” Aang said optimistically.

Katara bit her lip nervously, “I hope-”

A screech and plummeting silhouette interrupted the group’s conversation.

“Momo!” Aang shouted, chasing after the lemur that had just crashed into the snowbank. The animal shook itself out of its stupor, and climbed all over Sokka, starting to screech ridiculously loud.

“Wha- stupid lemur, get off of me! Why are you so hyper!”

“Guys…” Aang said slowly, “Momo was carrying… Lee’s stuff.”

Sokka looked around to see the stuff in question. Lee’s usual black supply bags were strewn across the snow.

“Why… do you… think maybe something happened to him?” Katara asked, nervous.

Sokka searched Momo for a letter but found nothing. “No letter… I’m sure he’s…”

“He’s gotta be fine, right?!” Aang began to freak.

“We should go get your bison!” Yue suggested, a bit calmer than the rest of them.

“Right,” Sokka agreed, “This could just be some sort of mistake… but it’s weird enough that I don’t care about the guards splashing me with water.”

“I mean they’d do a lot more than that…” Yue winced, but she ultimately followed as the group headed further into the ice city, towards the stable they had Appa stay in.

As all four of them speedily weaved through the streets, Katara abruptly stopped.

“Katara, what-”

“Shh!” She shut Aang up. She turned down a street, and Sokka could hear what she was being drawn to. Wet coughs echoed down the street where a dark figure sat on the edge of one of the canals. It looked like… the person came out of the water. No one’s insane enough to go into that water voluntarily!

Yue, Katara, and Aang must’ve thought the same thing because they began to approach the guy. The person shakily stood but puffed out a few weak flames from their mouth as they regained their balance.

*Wait a spirits-damned minute-!*

“Lee?!” Katara and Aang managed to yell at the same time.

Lee startled, but recovered and rushed towards them. He was soaking wet, and his raven-black hair clung to his face.

“O-oh, thank Agni, I needed to see you guys,” Lee spoke with a slight tremble, and the lisp he had was in full force. Somehow, he kept dodging Katara’s attempts to reach out and touch him. “Listen, I-I… I need you to t-trust me on this, the Fire N-Nation is *here* and they want to take out all the waterbenders- well, Zhao does, he’s leading the fleet, and- and, he wants the moon- but I just needed to *warn* you because they had *so many ships-* ”

Sokka interrupted him, “Lee! Buddy slow down, what do you mean the Fire Nation is-” Sokka stopped when he realized the firebender was looking past him. He turned, where Yue stood, also staring at Lee. She looked curious, and Lee just looked dazed.

“Ha, so she isn’t old…” Lee muttered before his eyes rolled back along with his head. Everyone cried out as Aang launched forward and caught the now unconscious firebender by the armpits. It was then that Sokka finally caught a good view of his face.

“Tui and La, what happened to him?!”

“I don’t know,” Katara said, kneeling to assess their friend. She was in what Sokka mentally called *‘healer mode.* “His breathing isn’t... right. It’s erratic. Is there anywhere we can lay him down and I can have a look?”

Yue stepped forward a bit, “I can go make sure there’s a healing room available-”

“Somewhere more… private?” Katara asked, “We don’t need them to... throw him out again, definitely not while he’s obviously injured. He needs to be a... secret for now.”

“But Katara, he said something about the Fire Nation attacking. Shouldn’t we warn someone?” Aang questioned.

“We can go tell Master Pakku in the morning. Where should we take him *now?* ”

“Let’s just put him in my room,” Sokka said, “I stay there most of the time, so no one would randomly stumble upon,” He gestured to Lee’s passed out form, “... this, not without me knowing.”

“Okay, Sokka could you carry him?”

Sokka was too worried to complain. “Sure.”

Once Lee was hefted onto Sokka, they made their way towards the place Katara, Aang, and Sokka shared. Yue had to leave, she didn’t want to get in trouble with her father for being out so late.

Sokka felt Lee’s uneven breath on the side of his neck. He wasn’t sure if it gave him reassurance or uneasiness.

Lee’s touch was usually always warm with comfort, now all he felt was dreadful cold. Sokka couldn’t bring himself to stop frowning.

---

Zuko woke slowly to the sound of rhythmic snoring. With a groan, he tried to move but found that the snoring sound came from a heavyweight on his thigh. Sokka was…

Sleeping on his thigh. Drooling on his thigh.

Zuko shook his leg around, and Sokka shot up with a confused, “Huh!” He gasped upon seeing Zuko staring at him, and yelled, “Katara! Lee’s awake! He’s awake!”

The boy maneuvered towards the firebender, a grin covering his previously relaxed and drooling face. “Lee, buddy, you’re awake! How do you feel?!”

“Uh,” Zuko rasped, his ‘I just woke up’ voice scratching his throat uncomfortably, “Like I got ran over by a merchant cart?”

“Wrong answer,” Katara sighed from the doorway, “Move over, Sokka.” She spoke to Zuko now, “I tried healing your ribs already but I guess it must’ve not worked… let me try again-”

Zuko poked himself in the ribs, the action surprisingly not making him flinch or twinge. “I think it worked,” He said, “I’m just tired, that’s all.”

“To be fair, Katara, he does still look like he got run over by a merchant cart,” Sokka chuckled.

Katara frowned, “Well now that it’s the morning I can at least heal his face.”

Zuko pushed himself into a better sitting position, putting some distance between himself and Katara’s outstretched hands. “It’s fine, it doesn’t hurt. What happened last night?”

“You uh… sorta started to tell us that the Fire Nation is preparing to attack before passing out. Katara figured out you had broken some of your ribs and she healed them,” Sokka recounted. Zuko blinked, his usual resolve slowly coming back to him.

“What’s the plan to counter the Fire Nation’s attack? Where’s Aang, is he-”

“Woah, woah! Calm down dude,” Sokka pushed Zuko down from his attempt to stand, “We couldn’t exactly tell anybody what you told us, but Aang’s heading to Master Pakku now to tell him. Right now, you’re…”

“You’re a secret,” Katara finished, “We didn’t think anything good would happen if we told someone you broke into the city… wait, how’d you even get in?!”

“Turtle-seal tunnels,” Zuko said off-handedly, crossing his arms in thought. He wasn’t sure how much time the North had until Zhao’s fleet reached their walls, or what secrets they knew about the moon spirit that Zhao apparently thought he could destroy-

“I know he did not just say turtle-seal tunnels,” Sokka wheezed with disbelief, “Katara, my ears must be broken, can you heal them because I know there’s no way I just heard that correctly.”

Katara ignored her brother, “I- Lee, you went through… what?”

“The tunnels that the turtle-seals use, they go straight to the city’s waterways. Did you not know…?”

“Lee,” Katara said, looking a bit exasperated, “We know. But there’s no way a human could swim through those tunnels, turtle-seals can hold their breath for over twenty minutes!”

Sokka ran a hand over his hair, “Well, it’s no wonder he broke his ribs then,”

“I’m pretty sure that’s not how that works…” Zuko said, “And that’s not how I fractured-”

“-Broke,” Katara interrupted.

“-My ribs,” He blinked, “... They were broken?”

“A few, yes,” Katara said, “What could’ve possibly broken them then?”

“I got caught up in an explosion.”

Katara threw her hands into the air. Sokka facepalmed.

---

“So,” Sokka said, inhaling in preparation to speak, “Let’s get this straight. You were using too much of your energy firebending to stay warm, so you decided to find the open ocean. When you got there, you found Zhao’s ship anchored with a fleet behind him. You snuck onto the ship, dressed as a soldier, listened into a briefing that told you basically all of Zhao’s psycho plan to kill a spirit that is disguised as a fish, and then just *quietly waltzed out* as the Blue Spirit. Then, you rushed to warn us but the pirates that Katara stole from that are now *apparently* working for Zhao saw your mask and made a *ball* of *explosives* to fling at you. It hit you, but somehow you didn’t drown, die, or, well, *explode* ! You woke up with three broken ribs instead! Once you were up and running, by some insane miracle, you made the trip back to the city by night time. After getting Momo to be your personal carrier, you decide, ‘Oh, how about I just follow these turtle-seals into their super tiny tunnels where they don’t breathe oxygen for minutes at a time and the temperature is below-freezing!’ and you *finally* get to your destination and give your warning only to pass out from exertion seconds later?!”

Zuko pouted. “Well, it sounds dramatic when *you* say it.”

“Because it’s the most *dramatic* series of events I’ve ever heard!”

“What’s dramatic? Lee! You’re awake!” Aang gasped, poking his head through the entrance. Zuko groaned and flopped back onto Sokka’s bedding. He tensed when he sensed another person walk into the room.

Master Pakku stared him down. Zuko felt a familiar scowl cross his own face.

“Avatar Aang has informed me of the warning you have given our tribe. However, you have broken into our city. I have-”

“Master Pakku,” Katara blurted, “Lee is my patient. I’d prefer it if you give him some space.”

“... I have reason to question him, pupil Katara. One on one. I assure you no one else in the tribe will be involved.”

“Uh, how about no sir,” Sokka scoffed, standing and crossing his arms. Zuko stuck his palm out against the boy’s leg, stopping him from advancing towards the older man.

“It’s fine, really,” Zuko said carefully, “I’m willing to talk.”

“Lee, you really don’t have to-”

“Sokka, what’s the worst that could happen?”

Katara frowned. “Of all people, you are not allowed to say that.”

The siblings left, Aang in tow. Before Pakku could open his mouth, Zuko searched and grabbed the lotus tile that had miraculously stayed in his pocket.

“Do you recognize this?!”

Pakku snatched it out of his hand and began to inspect it.

“Hey, give that back!” Zuko snapped.

“Where did you get this?”

The firebender frowned. “If you recognize it you should know,” He added for good measure, “... sir.”

Pakku turned his nose up. “Do you really think this little piece of wood can prove your innocence? Or was your Uncle just too air-headed to remind you how to keep a secret?”

“Don’t talk about Uncle like that!” Zuko sneered before realizing, “So you do know?”

The waterbender tossed the tile back to Zuko. “Stop speaking so openly about this. This is the exact reason we don’t include children, you know. Last I heard you were still loyal to your father. I can’t trust you.”

Zuko paused. “You- Listen, I don’t even know what the stupid tile means, Uncle just told me he had friends all over the world and… well, you look old enough,” His mouth quirked a bit when Pakku bristled, offended, “And when was the last time you ‘heard’ of me? What does that even mean?!”

“War is about making allies,” Pakku sighed, “Last I went out to hear from any of my own was almost three years ago.”

“People can change in three years, sir.”

“I’m well aware, seeing as you are not trying to go after the Avatar.”

Zuko tapped his fingers on his leg nervously. “So… Uncle communicates with his allies using the lotus tile… and he told you about our situation three years ago,” He tried not to feel a little mad that Uncle felt the need to give Zuko’s story out like candy, but that was a separate matter to deal with. “Why would his allies need to know our status?”

Master Pakku watched him for a few seconds before turning away.

“Allies often need a leader. Why would you need to use a fake name?”

*I’m never going to know what Uncle’s the leader of, am I?*

Zuko clenched his fists. “You see the way your tribe reacted to the way I look? Imagine if they knew my name, my bloodline. Allies don’t need to share everything about themselves, sir.”

“... I suppose that’s true.”

“And I don’t care if you trust me or not, I’m trying to help you! The Fire Nation is on your front doorsteps and you don’t seem to have a plan,” Zuko grit out. He was tired of this old people nonsense when there were much larger things to worry about.

“And who brought them here, *Lee?* ” Pakku asked, still facing away from the former prince.

“Wha- a man named Admiral Zhao, sir.”

“And how do you know his plan?”

“Because I snuck onto his ship, why are you… you think I led them here!”

Pakku turned on him, “You are the Fire Nation prince, why wouldn’t you?!”

“I’m not the idiot who wants to kill the damned moon spirit!” Zuko roared, standing to defend himself.

Pakku took a breath. “I will not trust you unless you go up against your own people-”

“I *have* been going against my own people!” Zuko felt himself shake with rage. “I’m not a naive child, and I don’t have to prove *anything* to *you* . You should worry about preparing yourself for a brutal frontal attack instead of worrying about someone who’s trying to help you. Now, I’m going to find *my* allies, and you will not say a word, because I don’t listen to people who throw their allies out into the freezing snow!”

He pushed past Pakku and ducked under the animal skin that the Northerners used as an entrance to their buildings. He hadn’t realized he’d been so worked up about… everything.

Zuko spared a glance at where the horizon should be, where the huge ice wall resided. Any moment, smoke would seep from over it and the Fire Nation would unleash their wrath on the glittering city of ice. He hated the quiet before a fight.

Placing a steadying hand on his chest, Zuko quieted the hammering of his heart.

“Lee!”

Zuko turned and saw Sokka bounding towards him. The teen grabbed Zuko’s biceps and shook him a bit. “So, what did Pakku say? Do I need to sock him?”

Sokka made little circles with his fists, mocking a fistfight.

“He’s… a real asshole. Can’t believe Katara tolerates him.”

“She barely does, man,” Sokka shook his head, “Anyway, come with me!”

“Wha-! Where are we going?!” Zuko questioned, trying his best to ignore the people along the water canals staring at him and the boy who was dragging him by clasping their hands together.

“I’m meeting with Princess Yue today! She wanted to meet you, but like properly this time!”

As they approached a much quieter, inner part of the city, Zuko saw Sokka wave excitedly and call out to an elegantly-dressed girl that sat on the steps of a very pretty ice bridge.

After catching his breath, Sokka gestured back and forth between Zuko and Yue. The princess stood, back straight just like a royal should. Zuko had gotten enough whacks to the spine to know that kind of posture could only belong to royalty.

“It’s a pleasure to meet you,” Yue spoke softly, “Sokka has told me a lot about you.”

“Uh,” Zuko ignored the heat he felt on his face from that particular comment and bowed his head, embarrassed that he didn’t know the correct bow he should use in front of the princess. Sokka stood beside his friends, a tight smile on his face that told Zuko, *‘If you embarrass me I will* *clobber you with my boomerang.* ’

“... He’s told me much about you as well. Nice to meet you.”

A short awkward silence followed, with Yue glancing at Sokka. The boy sighed and seemed to have a mental conversation with the princess before nodding.

The white-haired girl began to almost… tremble with curiosity, with the way her eyes got wide and excited. “Is it true you’re a firebender?”

“... Yes.”

“Could I see it?”

Zuko looked at Sokka helplessly. “Since she found out I was friends with a firebender she wanted to see firebending in action,” He laughed.

“W-well, it’s just that I’ve never seen any other benders,” Yue reasoned, “Every time I ask the elders they just say *‘No bending is as elegant as waterbending,’* ... so I’m just curious.”

Zuko swallowed the lump in his throat. He could definitely relate to the effects of bending superiority, though the Fire Nation was a lot harsher about it.

You know. Starting a war over their greatness and all.

He shook his head to clear away his thoughts.

“You don’t have to,” Princess Yue added, “It was rude to ask right after you were injured and all.”

“No… it’s fine. I just wasn’t expecting you to ask.”

Zuko outstretched his hand and faced his palm to the sky. A small flickering flame sprouted. He wasn’t going to take the risk of doing some cocky combative move in this setting. Yue and Sokka gathered around his hand.

“Do the trick you showed me under that waterfall in South Mali,” Sokka whispered.

Zuko nodded, obediently closing his hand softly before twisting a bit, making the small flame he had produced spring outwards into a shape that distinctly looked like a firelily, the ends of the petals being a bright red. He only perfected this particular shape from being cooped up in the tea shop too long, homesick.

Princess Yue gasped in wonder and Zuko glanced over to Sokka, who was making goo-goo eyes at her through Zuko’s fire. The firebender felt as if he was the table between two lovers on a romantic date.

What had Suki said? He was a good ‘wingman’?

Well, he couldn’t let his reputation die, could he?

Once again, he brought the fire down before jutting his hand out quickly, making small, crackling bursts of sparks that looked like fireworks. He quietly congratulated himself on being able to make the bursts different colors for once, that was new.

“Wow! Fire can be different colors?!”

“Lee’s can, although you still never told us why,” Sokka answered Yue, lightly punching Zuko’s arm.

“I just… can.”

“But c’mon, that seems impossible! Usually, colored fire only comes from a reaction when the element coming in contact with other substances, for example, potassium can make purple, barium makes green, sodium makes this deep orange color-”

Yue giggled at Sokka’s little rant. Sokka didn’t pout.

*Oh Agni. He didn’t pout when she interrupted him. Someone’s down bad…*

“What happened to thinking bending is *magic* , Sokka?” Zuko teased, “Where’d you even learn all that anyway? I don’t even know what bar… barry…”

“Barium. It’s a chemical. Believe it or not, despite our small population the South Pole still retains a lot of our math and science scrolls. Not a lot of literature, because we keep stories through oral tradition, but I must’ve read every scroll my dad let me get my hands on.”

“So, is chemistry your favorite subject?” Yue asked.

Zuko mentally groaned. He pushed every memory of any type of chemistry down into an unattainable hole in his brain. He was fine with math, great with literature, but science either elicited an existential crisis or an hour or two’s worth of completely zoning out.

“Eh. I always liked the more mathematical stuff, especially geometry. Unless I could make an explosion, chemistry was boring.”

*More like brain-numbing,* Zuko thought.

“So,” Yue once again turned to Zuko, “Lee, you can make colorful fire without any outside materials? That’s so cool…”

“It’s nothing. I hope I could make your first look at firebending… a little nicer than it might've been, Princess Yue,” Zuko said, grimacing as his mind swept him away from this meager moment of just three teenagers discussing their favorite school subjects. There was still an invasion slowly creeping up on them, and Zuko could only hope Pakku actually listened to his words.

“Are you kidding?” She smiled, “There’s so much I’ve never seen- or even heard of! Colored fire, airbending, air bison, volcanos...” Yue said the next part quietly like it was a secret she’d only shared with the stars, “One day… one day I’d like to see the whole world,”

“Travelling is probably the best part of being buddies with the Avatar,” Sokka smirked, “Although, being attacked left and right ruins most of the sightseeing.”

Sokka paused before lighting up. “Hey, maybe we could convince your father to let you come with us! We still have to find an earthbending teacher for Aang.”

“I’d love to see earthbending up close as well,” Yue let out a breath, “But my father would never allow that.”

“Hmm… well, I can be very convincing…” Sokka paused, stopping himself from getting closer to the princess (who he was already *very* close to), probably realizing Zuko was still awkwardly standing there.

“Lee,” Yue said nicely, despite being a bit tense, “Could Sokka and I talk alone for a minute?”

“Go ahead,” Zuko shrugged, “Do you guys know where Aang is?”

Sokka pointed him to the nearest waterbending training grounds. “Should be off around there, although warning; he’s a pretty awful slacker.”

“Trust me, I, of all people, know that. It was nice to meet you, Princess.”

“Likewise. And it’s just Yue.”

Zuko nodded before leaving the couple alone. He heard a short whisper of ‘old lady?’ before fully turning away.

---

“Lee!”

“Hi, Aang. How’s… waterbending going?”

“Great! We just finished our morning session and Katara has everyone totally beat,” The Avatar grinned, a small blush coating his face.

Zuko sighed. Seemed he was the third-wheel no matter where he went.

“Oh, Lee there you are,” Katara came up behind Aang, “There’s going to be a huge meeting in a minute, are you coming?”

He didn’t have to ask what the meeting would be about, but he hadn’t been asked to be there to relay any information, so Zuko doubted any tribe members actually wanted him there.

“No.”

“I- okay. Wait- Aang, look outside!”

The three of them ventured outside, where snow was falling.

*Wait.*

Zuko had only seen snow a few times in his life. This *wasn’t* snow.

He outstretched his hand and a layer of black dots found their way to his palm. He smeared them across his fingers, the soot engraving itself into his skin.

“Black snow. This is black snow. Aang, let’s go,” Katara tugged at the Avatar’s arm, but Aang was spaced out, staring at the sky.  
“Let’s *go,* ” Katara dragged him away, towards the center of the city, Zuko being left behind.

---

“I can't do it ... I can't do it,” Aang panted, coming back from his small expedition beyond the now destroyed ice wall. Zuko had offered to go with him, but the younger boy had shoved him away. “What happened?” asked Katara.

Aang dully replied, “I must have taken out a dozen Fire Navy ships, but there's just too many of them. I can't fight them all.”

“But, you have to. You're the Avatar,” Princess Yue pleaded.

“I'm just one kid,” Aang pleaded back.

Zuko’s heart twisted uncomfortably. He squatted down to reach Aang’s eye level.

“You did what you could,” The firebender said gruffly, “Did you see how many ships there were?”

“I-I didn’t count.”

“What was their formation?”

“Huh? Oh, um… they were in a big ‘V’ shape, sort of,” Aang replied, making a V-shape with his hands to show Zuko.

“Great,” Zuko scoffed. He stood up and addressed Yue and Katara, the only people who were probably willing to listen to him at the moment. Other Water Tribe members were running amuck, probably doing what they thought was necessary to prepare for an attack.

“Seems they’re sticking to a brutal frontal attack,” He scowled.

“What does that mean?” Yue asked.

“It means Admiral Zhao’s cocky, and that they’re only coming in from the front. They expect to use force throughout the entire siege. Is there anywhere we can fall back to?”

“But… what about the city?”

“What about it?!” Zuko snapped, “It’s going to be invaded no matter what we do… and they want to come right now.”

“Why now? Wouldn’t it have been more effective if they attacked at night? Yesterday?” Katara asked, following Zuko as he went further into the city, Aang and Yue following close behind.

“Even Admiral Zhao isn’t stupid enough to attack a *Water* Tribe at *night* ,” He explained, “Firebenders are much more powerful with the sun out than they are when it has set. I’ve always risen with the sun.”

Zuko was well aware the sun was going down now. He was sure Admiral Zhao would resume a much more violent attack at daybreak.

“The legends say the moon was the first waterbender,” Yue muttered, “Our strength comes from the spirit of the moon. Our life comes from the spirit of the ocean. They work together to keep balance.”

“I’ve always noticed my waterbending is stronger at night,” Katara admitted.

Aang gasped, scaring Momo off his shoulder. “The spirits! Maybe I can find them and get their help!”

Zuko made a face. “What?”

The girls ignored him.

“How can you do that?” Yue asked.

Katara excitedly smiled. “The Avatar is the bridge between our world and the Spirit World! Aang can talk to them!”

Yue smiled in understanding. “Maybe they'll give you the wisdom to win this battle!”

It took a lot for Zuko to hold his tongue. They really were going to rely on the spirits to survive this?

Yue told the group to follow her. They came upon a circular wooden door.

Zuko was going insane, there should be no way grass could just grow in the middle of the Northern Water Tribe… That is what he would say if he wasn’t seeing it with his own eyes. A rush of *warm* air reached Zuko fingertips and nose and *oh he wasn’t going to question this if it meant he could be* *warm again-*

The group made their way to a small oasis. Aang casually sat and began to try and meditate, his arrows giving off a faint glow after a few minutes.

Zuko looked into the water and saw a flash of silver. He blinked and saw two unusually colored koi fish circled each other.

Zuko remembered Zhao’s words, *The oh-so-powerful moon spirit takes form as something as weak* *and helpless as a fish...*

“This is what Zhao’s looking for,” Zuko blurted, “He knows the moon spirit is… that fish.”

Yue stilled. “I- you did say he wanted to kill the moon spirit, didn’t you?”

“Yes. Shit, where’s Sokka?”

“He went with the other men to defend the city,” Katara said nervously.

Zuko’s eye twitched. He took a breath and took in the heat signatures of everyone in the area. He was completely on edge, unable to ask Sokka for help, knowing Yue wasn’t a fighter and Katara wouldn’t leave Aang’s side. It was so cold on the other side of the oasis, he couldn’t see over the ice that surrounded them.

There was only a breath of silence before Zuko heard something shuffle, and eight Fire Nation soldiers came down on the oasis.

Zuko yelled out and attacked the one that came for him. Two soldiers went towards Katara, who was using her newfound waterbending abilities to fight them off. Luckily, none went for the princess, who hid away safely.

Zuko let a fireblast hit the soldier attacking him and rushed to Aang, who three soldiers were reaching to grab. Zuko swung fire in an arc to make them back away, hefting Aang onto his back. He faintly heard Katara shout not to move Aang, but he had no choice.

“Give us the Avatar!” One of the masked soldiers shouted.

Zuko put his fists up and lit fire daggers. Why these soldiers weren’t going after the moon spirit, he didn’t know.

“Sorry,” He growled, “Avatar’s off-limits.”

Another soldier spoke in a low but dangerous voice. “The Avatar needs to be taken out, the Admiral will take the moon,”

Zuko felt a chill go down his spine. The three soldiers rushed at him, and he could only dodge, not wanting to hurt Aang. He looked up towards the mountainous wall of ice that led up to an unpredictable cold that went much beyond the Water Tribe city. And he knew what he had to do.

Katara sent a water whip towards the soldier closest to Zuko.

“I’m getting the heck out of here,” Zuko told her, using her water arms as a cover for himself, “They’re only after the Avatar, Zhao is the only one he’s allowed to kill the moon spirit. He is cowardly and pretty known for traveling behind his armies.”

“Are you sure?!” Katara grit out. Zuko nodded. “Go then! I can fight them off, but it’s going to take a minute!” She threw a soldier against the ground, “We’ll find you! Keep Aang safe!”

Zuko nodded again, pushing his way through two annoyingly tall soldiers. When they turned to reach for him and Aang, Katara blasted them with water, making them fall. Zuko ran.

---

Aang was a light kid, but trudging through snow that went up to Zuko’s thighs and winds that blew his mask and hood off his face made it beyond frustrating.

Zuko felt the familiar surge of anger rise in his chest. Who was he angry at? Admiral Zhao for being an absolute nuisance? Master Pakku for not taking him seriously?

Himself?

He ran from a fight, leaving Katara to fight alone. Obviously, she could defend herself, and no soldier was going to attack the ocean and moon spirits unless they wanted to invoke Admiral Zhao’s wrath, and taking Aang away from the scene was the safest option.

So why did he feel so agitated, as if fire ants were crawling under his skin?

Zuko cursed as the ice he stood on began to crack. He began to run, hoping he could escape the ice caving into itself. The rush of pressure threw him a few feet away, sending him and Aang sprawling into the snow.

*Seriously, how is he still unconscious?!*

The firebender glanced around, as he had now lost his sense of direction. Everything was just a mess of white and gray, white and gray…

A cave entered his view. “Shelter…” He muttered, rushing to heft the Avatar onto his shoulders.

---

“There’s no way they’ll actually find us in this mess,” Zuko grumbled. A fucking blizzard. He’d never seen one before, but now he thinks ‘blizzard’ is too light a word to describe the flurries of malicious snow that brushed every corner of his vision with a violent white. “There’s always something,” He sighed, shaking his head in dismay. He wasn’t sure who he was talking to, the snow, or the unconscious Avatar behind him.

“If you were awake, I’m sure we wouldn’t be stuck in this blizzard. I’m not sure you understand running from a fight. It’s not something people like you have to do.”

Zuko ran a hand through his hair, his fingers trembling not from cold but from the gross self-loathing that found its way into his mind.

“You’re like my sister,” He scoffed, “Without the scary, threatening me if I look at her wrong part. Everything came easy to her. She’s a firebending prodigy, and everyone adores her. Or at least, they act like they do. My…”

Zuko glanced back at the Avatar, the boy he was supposed to wrap in chains, the *last* airbender. He was still breathing softly, showing no signs of waking up.

“My… father. He always said my sister was born lucky. And me? I was lucky to be born. As if that was luck… whatever. I don’t need luck. I don’t even want it. I’ve always had to struggle and fight…”

*But did you?*

“... And that’s what made me strong. It…”

*You gave up. You were weak. You couldn’t take it anymore, you gave up. You gave up, you ran* *from the fight.*

“... It’s made me who… who I am,” Zuko finished, staring down at his hands, his fingertips contrasting the snow with bright red from the cold.

Who was he? Was he Zuko, Prince of the Fire Nation, who never gave up? Who was loyal? Or was he the lousy teaboy who sat on his ass instead of doing something? Instead of picking a side? Instead of fighting?

A shaky breath left him, and he began to speak without caring that he didn’t know what was coming out of his mouth.

“I’m such a *coward* ,” He croaked, “Everything I do… it’s like every step I take forward is two steps back. A-and every time I look at you, at your crazy tattoos and colorful clothing it’s like I’m *right back there,* burning the corpses of people who never should’ve died. People my bloodline killed. I hide behind a mask so I don’t have to deal with people only seeing the worthless prince I was always made out to be, and the only time I’m happy is when I pretend to have another name!”

Zuko’s jaw tightened. He closed his eyes, remembering Uncle’s words, the ones that rang in his ears every time he smiled and laughed with his new friends.

*“I’m not sure you’re happy, like this,”* He had said.

Zuko stared out into the snowstorm.

“I don’t even think I’m happy,” He said miserably, “Not when I’m pretending to be Lee.”

---

“Tui and La, your fingers are freezing! What did I tell you about mittens?!”

“Firebenders don’t wear mittens,” Zuko grumbled, letting Sokka pull him onto Appa. Aang had luckily woken up and not too long after Katara, Yue, and Sokka had found them.

“I knew you’d be fine,” Sokka sighed, his tone serious, though Zuko doubted he’d joke too much during an active siege. “You never give up.”

“Not to some stupid snow,” Zuko replied, ignoring his blush. It was just the cold. He cupped his hands around his mouth and willed out fire, but a gasp broke him out of concentration.

“He really can breathe fire?!” Yue exclaimed. Zuko awkwardly nodded, resuming his method of warming up. Everything was pretty quiet for a minute.

Until the moon turned *fucking red.*

---

*Now I know why her hair is white,* was Zuko’s first thought after Yue told her story and connection to the moon spirit.

---

“Zhao, don't!” Aang cried out.

Zuko cursed every spirit Uncle had ever taught him the name of. Zhao got there first. He held the moon spirit in his hand.

“It's my destiny to destroy the moon and the Water Tribe,” Zhao raved. He looked insane, which was unsurprising because he was.

“Destroying the moon won't hurt just the Water Tribe. It will hurt everyone, including you. Without the moon, everything would fall out of balance. You have no idea what kind of chaos that would unleash on the world!” Aang shouted desperately.

“He’s right Zhao,” Zuko cautioned, knowing damn well what would happen to the Fire Nation’s islands if the Admiral got his wish.

“And who might you be?”

Zuko suddenly was *very* aware he didn’t have his mask. His full face was exposed to Zhao yet… the older man didn’t recognize him. Somehow, that made him even angrier.

He snarled, “Whatever you do to that spirit I'll unleash on you ten-fold. Let it go, now!”

Zhao stilled, looking between the squirming fish and his hand, then between the water and the sky. It was quiet on both sides.

The quiet before the storm.

As all color bled from the atmosphere besides the sickly orange Zhao had sliced upon the great spirit.

Zuko let the anger that had been building in his chest free. He rushed at Zhao and his men, taking out the three soldiers accompanying him with quick and powerful fire blasts. He turned his attention to the Admiral, who was still backing away from his crime in shock.

Zuko hoped he was regretting it.

Their chase began, the younger firebender pushing the thought of his friends behind in his pursuit of the Admiral. Zuko kept throwing punches as quickly as Zhao could dodge them, and the look of malice returned to the older man’s face.

They rounded into an abandoned part of town, the jabs of fire glittering hauntingly across the gray, lifeless ice.

“Why are *you* still attacking me?!” Zhao demanded, “I don’t even know who you are!”

“You will!” Zuko shifted his stance, using his legs to fire at Zhao. He lit his palms as the Admiral scrambled and tripped over the stairs to a meticulously constructed ice bridge that hung over a gaping water canal.

As his rage took his features, Zuko’s rage took his flames as well.

Green and red.

Guilt and rage.

Zhao’s eyes widened.

“Y-you're the Blue Spirit, an enemy of the Fire Nation!”

The older firebender seemed to fight harder after that realization, having a true grudge against Zuko now.

He still didn’t know why Zuko had a grudge against *him* though.

“Is that all?!” Zuko roared, sweeping Zhao’s legs out from under him. “Is that all you think I am?!”

He brought two flaming fists into a different stance, one fist shining unsteadily right near his face and the other bearing down on Zhao.

The man’s eyes flickered over the scar, and he looked up into Zuko’s golden eyes.

“... Zuko. And here I thought… you could never be more of a traitor,” He sneered at him, his twisted mouth only a cover for his shocked eyes. “You could’ve chosen to accept your defeat, all those years ago. I watched you cower and now you show up playing hero. You’re a disgrace, Zuko.”

“That’s Prince Zuko to you.”

The punches only got quicker and more dangerous from then. Two men with a grudge and different lives, different choices made. They fought as if they hated each other for decades when really they only could hate the thing the other represented.

Zuko saw nothing more than a heartless and cruel man. Zhao only saw a cowardly traitor.

The bridge was scuffed and blanketed in black streaks.

And suddenly the moon was lit again. Zhao yelled in protest, his place in history drowning.

Zuko looked around in shock as the water around the bridge began to glow, a thick tendril of water shooting up at the two firebenders. The young firebender dodged the blue glow as it snatched up Zhao, who struggled against it.

Frantically, Zuko’s body moved without his consent. He outstretched a hand to the captured Admiral, his subconscious knowing this wouldn’t end well.

Zhao looked at his show of aid and turned away.

Zuko watched him drown with pride, unable to produce any of his own.

---

The firebender trudged back to the oasis, fatigue clinging to his bones.

When he finally arrived, the moon was shining brightly into the oasis water, where Sokka was kneeling. He was staring at the moon. Katara stood behind him, hand over her mouth. Small, quiet tears disappeared on her face.

Sokka’s usually bright ocean eyes were dim and defeated, but if he had any tears, none fell.

Zuko sagged down onto the grass, crossing his legs before letting his head rest in his hands.

Like Sokka and Katara, his eyes never left the moon.

### Text

playlist here! reread and listen or enjoy on your own!!

---

Things didn’t go back to normal. Well, whatever normal was to the Avatar’s gang.

They had no real plan in mind, other than Aang needed an earthbending teacher. They didn’t have a particular person in mind, besides Bumi, but he was a king. Zuko had pointed out he could be busy and they should keep their options open. It felt weird, from having such a straightforward goal of getting to the North Pole to searching the large Earth Kingdom until someone caved into Aang’s incessant begging.

Katara had been ecstatic when Pakku revealed he was turning over a new leaf, that he was going to help rebuild the Southern Water Tribe. And while Aang hadn’t mastered waterbending, Katara had, so she’d teach him whatever was left to know.

Before they left, Zuko watched Sokka speak with Chief Arnook. He couldn’t hear their conversation, but Zuko could tell recent events were continuously bothering Sokka and that conversation didn’t help much.

Which brought them to now.

It was their first night back camping, and Sokka kept poking the dying embers of their campfire. Katara and Aang were already asleep. Zuko sat, back against a tree, trying to scratch off some soot on his spirit mask. It needed a repaint, but part of Zuko didn’t want to get rid of the chipped and smudged parts.

“Are you going to go to sleep?” Sokka’s voice uncomfortably broke the silence of the forest.

“I wasn’t planning on it… you?”

Sokka didn’t turn to speak to him. “You should sleep.”

To Zuko, that didn’t sound like much of a suggestion. Sokka wanted him to sleep, but the firebender doubted it was out of concern for his health.

“Are… you sure? If you think someone needs to take watch then I can do it while you sleep-”

“Just go to bed, Lee.”

Zuko sighed. “You should sleep too.”

Sokka didn’t reply, and Zuko shuffled away from the campfire. Since he didn’t really own a sleeping bag, he took to sleeping in trees. When he was a little kid, he used to climb the tree in the gardens and nap up there, back when Azula was too fawned over to climb trees. Sometimes, his mother would find him and scold him for getting twigs and leaves in his hair, and then she’d brush them away, frowning.

*“A prince should keep his hair tidy, no?”*

Zuko ruffled his hair around. It was really long now, the ends brushing his shoulders ever so slightly. He looked up at a particularly sturdy-looking tree for any bird or pecker-squirrel nests and scaled the trunk when he found none.

Settling on a thick branch, Zuko stared into the darkness, as if it might gain consciousness and yell at him to sleep. Because as tired as he was, he knew he wasn’t going to be able to rest tonight.

Some shuffling and footsteps interrupted Zuko’s absent-minded knife twirling. He stood on the branch and looked over the campsite only to see Sokka walking in the opposite direction of where everyone slept.

On the other side of the forest was a meadow-like clearing that Appa hung around earlier, mowing as much grass as he could. Zuko could’ve just brushed it off as Sokka going to sleep on Appa because sometimes Aang and Katara would do that, but something in the back of his mind told him to follow the other teen.

*Agni, I’m just getting nosier and nosier by the day, aren’t I?*

Zuko followed Sokka using the trees for cover, scowling at himself when he rustled one too many leaves.

Just as Zuko predicted, Sokka trudged past Appa, the beast just barely opening its eye to watch him.

Zuko watched Sokka walk (more like drag) himself to a plush area of short grass and fall into himself, one hand clutching the clothing on his chest and the other running down his face as he slid to the ground.

A few seconds went by and Sokka began to tremble enough that Zuko could see it from his spot in the trees. Even from so far away, the firebender caught the hitched sobs that floated through the soft night breeze. Something in his chest twinged. He would normally just walk away from this, but something about the person breaking in front of him being Sokka forced a pull from his mind and body.

With a soft *thump* , Zuko landed in the grass. He noiselessly made his way over to Sokka, wincing as the hurt sounds his friend made got a little louder with every step. Not wanting to think too hard, Zuko quietly sat beside Sokka, crossing his legs gracefully. When Sokka noticed him, he choked a bit before trying his best to wipe his tears, but they just kept coming. Zuko didn’t move, didn’t try to talk or touch him. He just sat with his palm against the grass, staring up at the stars as Sokka tried to put himself together.

His tears only just prickling at his eyes now, and the dried ones beginning to make red trails on his cheeks, Sokka spoke.

“Why aren’t you in bed, man?” He rasped as if it hurt physically to talk.

“Couldn’t sleep. Heard you. I… I want to help?”

Sokka chuckled wetly. “There’s nothing to help.”

“There’s always something to help,” Zuko replied ominously, “And I don’t think you’re the kind of person to cry over nothing.”

It was silent for a moment.

“I was holding her, you know,” Sokka mumbled, and Zuko tried his best to hear the next part, “I wish I hadn’t been.”

Zuko turned his gaze to the moon, which only showed half of itself tonight. Or, herself, he guessed.

“I heard from Katara what happened, I… I’m sorry.”

“‘S not your fault.”

“It’s not yours either.”

Sokka glared at him. “I was supposed to protect her. That was my job.”

“Seems to me she didn’t need to be protected much,” Zuko said.

Another glare was shot at him.

“The princess made the choice herself, right?”

“Barely,” Sokka scoffed, before resting his chin on his knee. “Cheif Arnook… her *father* , he said… he said he’d known it would happen. And I just… how could he do *nothing?* She wasn’t-She couldn’t have been born only to die. That’s…”

“It shouldn’t have happened,” Zuko agreed, “But it did.”

“But it *fucking* did. I don’t- I should’ve done something, anything-”

“You did the most,” Zuko declared. Sokka gave him an incredulous look. “Did you not see the way she *looked* at you? I mean- I really think she saw you as the best thing in her life, Sokka. I know that look.”

Zuko knew it from when Uncle looked at Lu-Ten’s portraits, from when Kovah and Suki sat across from each other and sappily stole glances of each other when they thought the other one wasn’t looking. He’d seen it in the way Aang stared at Katara when she laughed. Zuko knew he never experienced much love, not in his sixteen years of life, but that made it all that much easier to notice on the faces of others.

A few more tears escaped the corners of Sokka’s eyes, dripping down and cupping his jaw.

“She was only sixteen,” He whispered, “I’m almost sixteen. You’re sixteen. That’s… it’s so young to…”

“Everyone deserves a bit more time,” Zuko exhaled, “But… she’s not really gone, is she?”

Sokka stilled. “What… what is that supposed to mean?”

“Your sister told me her soul saved the moon spirit, making Princess Yue the new moon spirit,” Zuko turned and gestured with his head towards the moon, “She’ll always be there now, in the sky…”

“Do you think she can see us? Hear us?” Sokka asked, and the firebender could detect a little desperation at the edges of his tone.

“I don’t see why not.”

Sokka slowly turned to face the moon. The pained furrow in his brow smoothed out, ever so slightly.

“Hey, Yue,” He breathed. Zuko allowed himself a small smirk.

Sokka continued. “I’m sorry I couldn’t protect you,” tears began to stream steadily down his face, but they were silent, “But… I mean… I know you were focused on your duties… you knew it had to happen, right?”

“... You were very brave,” Zuko said to the moon. He didn’t want Sokka to feel weird about talking to something that wouldn’t respond.

When Zuko had lost his mother, he’d visit the gardens. He’d eventually gotten the mother turtleduck to trust him slowly, and bit by bit he’d spill his secrets to her as if maybe, just maybe, this turtleduck still remembered his mother. That somehow, if he spoke to this animal, his mother would hear.

“Very,” Sokka nodded, “I miss you, and I wish you could’ve stuck around… so I could show you the world. But I guess you already see the whole thing, huh?”

The Water-Tribe boy sighed, and it sounded much more content. Then he looked down, staring at a spot between his feet.

“I hope I don’t forget you.”

“You won’t forget her,” Zuko tried to assure.

Sokka shook his head. “I… I’m going to forget what she looks like. It’s just what happens.”

“What she… oh. I get it.”

Sokka made a face. “How could you possibly get it?”

“I… you know, when someone’s gone for so long you just… forget their face. It’s… upsetting.”

Zuko only really remembered what Lu-Ten looked like because Uncle kept his picture up. Zuko never carried a picture of his mother around. And everyone at the palace acted like she never existed. One day he woke up, and every trace of her was gone. He’d bet there were portraits of her back on Ember Island, but he wasn’t allowed there and every portrait in the palace had been disposed of.

So, usually, if Zuko wanted to picture his mom, he’d think of Azula. He could remember her face a lot more, and maybe that was because she was still alive. He’d just imagine Azula had kinder eyes and a soft smile, and he could build up the woman everyone told him had never been around in the first place.

Everyone wanted Zuko to forget his mother, but Sokka sounded as if he didn’t have a choice in forgetting the one he lost.

“Yeah,” Sokka stared at Zuko, “Yeah I… after a while I just… I can’t remember people’s faces. I hate it. My… I feel like such a piece of shit sometimes, because I can’t… I can’t even remember what my own mother looks like. I just always put Katara’s face on an older woman and hope it’s right, but it never is. And… I won’t be able to forgive myself if I forget what Yue looks like. I just won’t.”

Zuko stared at the moon and thought about one of the few reasons he had promised himself he’d capture the Avatar and go back to the Fire Nation. He wanted to be closer to the memory of his mother. Maybe he’d be able to go back to Ember Island and retrieve her portraits.

“Tell you what,” Zuko suggested after a few beats of silence, “When I get enough money, we’ll visit a portraitist. There are a lot of them who specialize in unreferenced portraits, for… well, past loved ones… and basically you just give them a list of features… and they paint the person for you. I’ll commission one for you, and then you won’t forget the princess. You can have her right in your pocket if you’d like.”

Sokka stared at him long and hard. After a while Zuko thought he had said something wrong but-

The teenager pounced on Zuko latched him in a hug that sent them both tumbling on their backs. The firebender let out a small *‘oof’* as Sokka’s weight settled on top of him.

“Thank you, Lee,” Sokka whispered into the crook of Zuko’s neck.

And if he was blushing? Well, that was *nobody’s* business.

Sokka pushed himself up so that his hands were on the grass beside Zuko’s chest and his face was level with the other teen’s.

“You… have no idea how much that means to me. So thank you.”

“It’s nothing,” Zuko replied breathlessly.

Sokka stared down at Zuko for a few seconds, before his nose scrunched up and his eyes flickered away, that miserable expression back on his face.

“I can’t believe my first girlfriend turned into the moon.”

A beat passed.

“That’s rough buddy.”

Sokka looked back at him, gaping a bit, before absolutely *losing* it. His head dipped into Zuko’s chest and he howled with laughter.

It was a hundred times a better sound than the sobbing from earlier, even if Zuko didn’t know what was so funny.

Sokka rolled off of him, clutching his sides and snorting like a hyena-hawk. It took him a couple of minutes to calm down while Zuko fretted at his side, unable to see how what he said was this hilarious.

“-Oho, oh man…!” Sokka wheezed, “How are you so funny without trying?”

“Uh? Not sure,” Zuko said, a bit concerned.

They lied in the grass, stewing in that familiar comfortable silence between them.

An owl-squirrel hooted in the distance.

“Are you alright?” Zuko asked. He rarely broke the silence, but he needed to know if he actually helped his friend.

“I am. I’m… I’m okay. You?”

“If you’re okay, then so am I.”

“... Good.”

### End Notes
